I'm not really thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: When you're not doing these tapping or freezing behaviors, do you feel anxious, uncomfortable, or like something is wrong until you do them?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I don't really feel anxious about it, or uncomfortable. It's not... it's not like that.\n\nDoctor: When you're tapping the scanner or freezing, do you notice any thoughts or worries running through your mind—like something that feels wrong or needs to be done a certain way—or is it more just a physical habit that happens on its own?\n\nPatient: No... no thoughts or worries like that. My mind is just... kind of blank, I guess. It just happens on its own.\n\nDoctor: If you wanted to, do you think you could stop yourself from tapping the scanner or freezing, or does it feel like something you can't control even if you tried?\n\nPatient: I don't think I can stop it. I'm not really sure.\n\nDoctor: Before you tap the scanner or freeze, do you ever feel a buildup of tension, an uncomfortable sensation, or an urge in your body or mind that gets relieved when you do the behavior?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't really feel anything before it happens. It's not like an urge or... tension. I just... I keep getting stuck doing things that don't fit the moment. I don't... I haven't noticed if it's the exact same every time. It's just tapping.\n\nDoctor: When you tap the scanner or freeze, does it happen really quickly in just a second or two, or does it last longer—like several seconds or more?\n\nPatient: I don't really know how long it is.
